Or he even learned it from a certain Doctor, who through nineteen propositions raves against the Conception and the oath to defend it, and whom Portel most fully refutes in *respons. casuum moral: p. 2. cas. 32. per tot.* Not without merit does he shun the Portuguese Tribunal of the Faith, to which he would certainly be liable, if he were to utter these most audacious things among us. He supposes that this most pious *sententia*, and one proximate to faith, is judged superstitious by the Supreme Pontiffs, the Bishops, and the Councils; which no one of sound mind will doubt to be most false. If that *sententia* is not superstitious — nay, if it is licit, pious, and more probable than the contrary — assuredly the oath concerning the defending of it will not be superstitious. This *sententia* has many centuries; this oath counts many ages in the face of the whole world; and yet in no wise was the Church disturbed by it: nay rather it brought her quiet, because all contentions concerning that *sententia* are found to have been settled. If that oath were superstitious, without doubt the Church would prohibit it; for she would not suffer the whole Christian people to be defiled by this sin. Known to the Roman See are these our oaths, known to the Bishops, known to the Tribunals of the Faith, but down to these times in no wise reprobated. The very silence of holy Mother Church in a matter so well known, and so common to all the Academies, is enough for a tacit approbation. What the Church has hitherto not judged superstitious and sinful, Muratori pronounces. Wondrous liberty of genius, wondrous confidence! No danger to faith, no danger to religion, no harm to souls does this most safe *sententia* bring with it. I could bring forward innumerable Spanish traditions, which modern critics, however most erudite, unjustly reject, perhaps on account of the envy created against the Spaniards: I could bring forward the authorities of the ancient Fathers, which the adversaries interpret according to their own measure: I could adduce into the midst the Greek Monologium: it would be easy to review innumerable miracles, by which this devotion toward the Conception is wondrously and evidently confirmed. Yet I pass over all these things, which to the most pious Muratori will seem musty fictions, and briefly — not with a thick, crackling, and rugged heap of phrases, but by the scholastic method — I shall show that the aforesaid oath is licit and religious. |
| 135 Suadetur igitur 1. hac intrinseca ratione. Licitum, & religiosum est juramentum de tuenda veritate aliquo modo ab Ecclesia definita. Sic se habet prædictum juramentum. Igitur &c. Minor, in qua difficultas esse potest, concludenter probatur. Quia Ecclesia præcipiendo annuam celebrationem immaculatæ Conceptionis, assignata Missa, & Officio, concessisque indulgentiis pro devote illam colentibus, atque à Xisto IV. Constitutione *Grave nimis* edita declaratione, quod Ecclesia non celebrat Conceptionem dumtaxat spiritualem, fulminata in oppositum asserentes excommunicatione, aliquam veritatem circa immaculatam Conceptionem definit, sicut definit circa Nativitatem B. Virginis præcipiendo illius celebrationem, ut constat ex *D. Thom. 3. p. q. 27. art. 4.* Quo argumento utitur Cardinalis Bellarminus *tom. 1. Controv. de Cultu Sanctor. lib. 3. cap. 16.* per hæc verba: *Quod si ipse quæreret, unde habeamus B. Mariam ab ipsa Conceptione sanctificatam?.... Responderem, inde nos hoc didicisse, unde ipse didicit B. Mariam ab ipsa Nativitate sanctam fuisse. Asserit enim ibidem, ex eo quod Ecclesia jam tunc celebrabat B. Virginis ortum, se nihil dubitare, quin ortus ille sanctus fuerit; & nos ergo ex eo, quod Ecclesia celebrat festum Conceptionis, nihil dubitamus Conceptionem illam sanctam aliquo modo fuisse.* Optimum, & concludens argumentum: cum Ecclesia nec colat, nec possit colere festum rei non sanctæ; aliter erraret, fidelesque in re dogmatica deciperet; quod asserere impium est, & hæreticum: ergo in celebratione Conceptionis sub nomine Conceptionis includitur definitio alicujus veritatis circa sanctitatem Conceptionis: supra hanc autem tuendam licite, & religiosissime cadere potest juramentum. Hæc paritas à festo Nativitatis deducta confirmatur optime ex Decreto Pii V. in quo festum Conceptionis in omnibus, & per omnia coæquatur Nativitatis festo, ut testatur Nieremberg *in lib. de Perpet. Object. festi Conception. cap. 21.* Ad remque opportune facit Angelici Doctoris auctoritas 3. p. q. 27. art. 1. sic dicentis: *Ecclesia celebrat Nativitatem Beatæ Virginis; non autem celebratur festum in Ecclesia, nisi pro aliquo sancto, ergo Beata Virgo in ipsa sua nativitate fuit sancta.* Optarem rationem differentiæ à Muratorio audire, & quod nos edoceret quando Beata Virgo fuerit sanctificata, ut nasceretur sancta. | | 135 It is urged, therefore, first, by this intrinsic reason. Licit and religious is an oath concerning the defending of a truth in some manner defined by the Church. Such is the aforesaid oath. Therefore, etc. The minor, in which the difficulty can lie, is conclusively proved. Because the Church, by enjoining the annual celebration of the immaculate Conception, a Mass and Office having been assigned, and indulgences granted to those devoutly honoring it, and by the declaration issued by Sixtus IV in the Constitution *Grave nimis*, that the Church does not celebrate a Conception merely spiritual, excommunication having been fulminated against those asserting the opposite — defines some truth concerning the immaculate Conception, just as she defines concerning the Nativity of the Blessed Virgin by enjoining its celebration, as is evident from *D. Thom. 3. p. q. 27. art. 4.* Which argument Cardinal Bellarmine uses *tom. 1. Controv. de Cultu Sanctor. lib. 3. cap. 16.*, in these words: *But if he himself should ask, whence we have it that the Blessed Mary was sanctified from her very Conception?.... I should answer that we have learned this from that same source whence he himself learned that the Blessed Mary was holy from her very Nativity. For he asserts in the same place that, from the fact that the Church even then celebrated the birth of the Blessed Virgin, he in no wise doubts that that birth was holy; and we therefore, from the fact that the Church celebrates the feast of the Conception, in no wise doubt that that Conception was in some manner holy.* An excellent and conclusive argument: since the Church neither honors, nor can honor, the feast of a thing not holy; otherwise she would err, and would deceive the faithful in a dogmatic matter; which to assert is impious and heretical: therefore in the celebration of the Conception under the name of Conception there is included the definition of some truth concerning the sanctity of the Conception: and upon the defending of this an oath can licitly and most religiously fall. This parity deduced from the feast of the Nativity is best confirmed from the Decree of Pius V, in which the feast of the Conception is in all things and through all things made equal to the feast of the Nativity, as Nieremberg testifies *in lib. de Perpet. Object. festi Conception. cap. 21.* And to the purpose there opportunely makes the authority of the Angelic Doctor 3. p. q. 27. art. 1., thus speaking: *The Church celebrates the Nativity of the Blessed Virgin; but a feast is not celebrated in the Church except for some holy thing; therefore the Blessed Virgin in her very nativity was holy.* I should wish to hear from Muratori the reason of the difference, and that he should teach us when the Blessed Virgin was sanctified, so that she should be born holy. |
| 136 Quod aliqua veritas definiatur circa sanctitatem Conceptionis, indubium est à paritate memoratæ Nativitatis B. Virginis, S. Joannis Baptistæ, & festi cujuslibet Sancti indicti ab Ecclesia. Quænam verò sit definitio? Lis est inter DD. Et licet aliqui æqualem definitionis, ac certitudinis gradum Conceptioni tribuant, ac Nativitati, id magis pie, quam theologice dictum fateor, attentis Constitutionibus Xisti IV. Alexandri VII. & S. Pii V. ut late probant Valentia *3. p. disp. 2. q. 1. n. 2.* Suarius *tom. 2. disp. 30. sect. 6.* Vasquius *tom. 2. disp. 117. cap. 5.* Negari tamen non potest, Ecclesiam celebrando Conceptionem proprie talem B. Virginis omnino definire doctrinam asserentem B. Virginem immunem à peccato originali, magis piam, & magis tutam esse. Ita Ægidius de Præsentatione *tract. de Concept. lib. 3. q. 6. art. 1.* P. Salazar *de Conceptione cap. 43. §. 1.* Cardinalis Bellarminus *tom. 4. controv. de amission. grat. lib. 4. cap.* 15. 16. & 17. ubi sententiam de Conceptione immaculata B. Virginis asserit contrariâ longe probabiliorem. Ergo juramentum de tuenda hæc veritate, in quo sensu à doctis usurpatur, est licitum, & religiosum, utpote cadens supra veritatem ab Ecclesia definitam. Ergo non citra temeritatem, & scandalum superstitiosum dicitur, & illicitum. | | 136 That some truth is defined concerning the sanctity of the Conception is beyond doubt from the parity of the aforementioned Nativity of the Blessed Virgin, of St. John the Baptist, and of the feast of any Saint whatever appointed by the Church. But what is that definition? There is a dispute among the Doctors. And although some attribute to the Conception an equal degree of definition and of certitude as to the Nativity, I confess that this is said more piously than theologically, the Constitutions of Sixtus IV, Alexander VII, and St. Pius V being attended to, as Valentia *3. p. disp. 2. q. 1. n. 2.*, Suárez *tom. 2. disp. 30. sect. 6.*, Vásquez *tom. 2. disp. 117. cap. 5.* amply prove. Yet it cannot be denied that the Church, by celebrating the Conception properly so called of the Blessed Virgin, altogether defines the doctrine asserting the Blessed Virgin to be immune from original sin to be the more pious and the more safe. So Ægidius de Præsentatione *tract. de Concept. lib. 3. q. 6. art. 1.*, Fr. Salazar *de Conceptione cap. 43. §. 1.*, Cardinal Bellarmine *tom. 4. controv. de amission. grat. lib. 4. cap.* 15. 16. & 17., where he asserts the *sententia* concerning the immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin to be far more probable than the contrary. Therefore an oath concerning the defending of this truth, in the sense in which it is employed by the learned, is licit and religious, as falling upon a truth defined by the Church. Therefore not without temerity and scandal is it called superstitious and illicit. |
| 137 Quod Ecclesia non solum definire, & declarare possit doctrinam aliquam esse de fide certam, sed esse magis probabilem, & tutam, ita ut in hac declaratione errare nequeat, docent Sotus *lib. 2. de Nat. & Grat. cap. 17. & 18.* Item *in 4. dist. 1. q. 3. art. 1.* Canus *de Sacramentis 4. p. post conclus. 5. & lib. 7. de locis cap. 2.* P. Vasques 1. 2. disp. 86. Turrianus *in 2. 2. D. Thom. disp. 14. dub. ult. & disp. 56. dub 9.* Arrubal *q. 11. art. 2.* quod probant exemplis, inter quæ illud notissimum ex *Clement. un. de Sum. Trinit. & Fid.* ubi Pontifex declarat probabiliorem eorum doctrinam, qui sentiunt, parvulis in Baptismo infundi habitus fidei, spei, & charitatis. Neque Pontifices permitterent eam prædicari, defendi, aut publice doceri, indicto silentio contrariis assertoribus, ni ea sententia tuta, tutior, & tutissima esset. Recogitet corde Muratorius quam sententiam dicat incertam, errori obnoxiam, opinioni tantum humanæ innixam, & pene indefinibilem. | | 137 That the Church can not only define and declare some doctrine to be certain of faith, but to be more probable and safe, in such wise that in this declaration she cannot err, is taught by Soto *lib. 2. de Nat. & Grat. cap. 17. & 18.*, likewise *in 4. dist. 1. q. 3. art. 1.*, Cano *de Sacramentis 4. p. post conclus. 5. & lib. 7. de locis cap. 2.*, Fr. Vásquez 1. 2. disp. 86., Turrianus *in 2. 2. D. Thom. disp. 14. dub. ult. & disp. 56. dub 9.*, Arrubal *q. 11. art. 2.*; which they prove by examples, among which is that most well known one from *Clement. un. de Sum. Trinit. & Fid.*, where the Pontiff declares more probable the doctrine of those who hold that in Baptism the habits of faith, hope, and charity are infused into little ones. Nor would the Pontiffs permit it to be preached, defended, or publicly taught, silence having been enjoined upon the assertors of the contrary, unless that *sententia* were safe, safer, and safest. Let Muratori consider in his heart what *sententia* he calls uncertain, liable to error, resting upon merely human opinion, and well-nigh indefinable. |
| 138 Hoc fundamento sic inconcusse stabilito, cum Sixtinam Constitutionem præ manibus habeamus, operæ pretium erit demonstrare, Muratorium in pœnas ibi, & in Constitutione Alexandri VII. *Solicitudo* declaratas, proculdubio incurrisse. Ipse opinionem de immaculata Virginis Conceptione errori obnoxiam, ac opinionis humanæ fallacissimæ metas non transgredi imperterritus, tumidusque asserit: hanc nostram devotionem pervicacem, & superstitiosam sui securus affirmat; & illiciti juramenti nos omnes, totque insignes Doctores reos constituit, & per consequens peccati lethalis macula inustos supponit. Quo posito, Xistus IV. inter alia hæc habet: *Sane cum sancta Romana Ecclesia de intemeratæ,* (nota adjectivum *intemeratæ,* quo utitur S. P. cujus synonyma sunt, intaminata, illibata, integra, pura, immaculata, quo nomine postea utitur paulo infra) *semperque Virginis MARIÆ Conceptione publice festum solemniter celebret, & speciale, ac proprium super hoc Officium ordinaverit,* (en ad suam Constitutionem idem fundamentum, quod supra assumpsimus) *nonnulli, ut accepimus, diversorum Ordinum prædicatores.... illos, qui tenent, aut asserunt eamdem gloriosam, & immaculatam Dei Genitricem absque originalis peccati labe fuisse conceptam, mortaliter peccare.... Qui eam sine hujusmodi macula conceptam esse affirmant, peccare graviter.* Arbitror non rectam posse assignari differentiæ rationem inter simplicem, & juratam assertionem; si enim licita est assertio, etiam licitum est juramentum ipsam respiciens assertionem, & è contra. Qui ergo tale juramentum illicitum, & peccaminosum adstruit, etiam illicitam assertionem, supra quam juramentum cadit, asserere visus est. | | 138 This foundation having been thus unshakenly established, since we have the Sixtine Constitution in hand, it will be worth the labor to demonstrate that Muratori has beyond doubt incurred the penalties declared therein, and in the Constitution *Solicitudo* of Alexander VII. He himself, undaunted and puffed up, asserts the opinion concerning the immaculate Conception of the Virgin to be liable to error, and not to transgress the bounds of a most fallacious human opinion: secure of himself, he affirms this our devotion to be obstinate and superstitious; and he constitutes us all, and so many distinguished Doctors, guilty of an illicit oath, and consequently supposes us branded with the stain of deadly sin. This being posited, Sixtus IV has among other things these words: *Since indeed the holy Roman Church, concerning the Conception of the undefiled,* (note the adjective *undefiled*, which the Holy Father uses, whose synonyms are: unstained, unsullied, whole, pure, immaculate — by which last name he afterwards uses a little below) *and ever Virgin MARY, publicly and solemnly celebrates a feast, and has ordained a special and proper Office upon this,* (behold, for his Constitution the same foundation which we assumed above) *certain preachers of divers Orders, as we have received.... [assert] that those who hold or assert that the same glorious and immaculate Mother of God was conceived without the stain of original sin, sin mortally.... that those who affirm her to have been conceived without such a stain, sin gravely.* I judge that no right reason of difference can be assigned between a simple and a sworn assertion; for if the assertion is licit, the oath regarding that same assertion is also licit, and conversely. He therefore who maintains such an oath to be illicit and sinful has been seen to assert illicit also the assertion upon which the oath falls. |
| 139 Certum perinde est hanc Muratorii doctrinam toti Regno, universis Hispaniis scandalum maximum adduxisse; quod quidem omnibus modis vitare studuit Xistus IV. per hæc verba: *Nos igitur hujusmodi temerariis ausibus, ac perversis assertionibus, quæ exinde in Ecclesia Dei exoriri possunt, quantum nobis ex alto conceditur, obviare volentes, motu proprio.... Qui affirmare præsumerent, eos qui crederent,* (nota, etiam prohibere affirmare, credentes immaculatam Conceptionem peccatum incurrere) *aut tenerent eamdem Dei Genitricem ab originalis peccati macula in sua Conceptione præservatam fuisse, propterea alicujus hæresis labe pollutas fore, vel mortaliter peccare.... utpote falsas, & erroneas, & à veritate penitus alienas, editosque desuper libros prædictos id continentes quoad hoc auctoritate Apostolica tenore præsentium reprobamus, & damnamus.* Et postea excommunicationis sententiam Sedi Apostolicæ reservatam inurit omnibus, qui sententias circa immaculatam Conceptionem veras non esse asserere ausi sint. Quid aliud attentat Muratorius? Expresse asserit eam *falsam esse posse.* Qui autem affirmat eam posse esse falsam, necessario negat eam esse veram; nam quæ vera est, falsitati non subest. Deinde Pontifex non prohiberet negare eam esse veram, si judicaret eam falsam esse posse. Ridiculi igitur sunt qui sentiunt, S. Pontificem nihil aliud constituisse, quam permittere prædictam sententiam ut piam, & probabilem; cum eam ita judicet veram, ut tam luculenter prohibeat asserere veram non esse. Si ergo vera est, & ut vera habenda sententia, quæ Deiparæ Conceptionem immaculatam defendit, licitum est etiam juramentum de hac veritate defendenda: proinde est illicita, reprobata, & Pontificiæ damnationi obnoxia ea sinistra opinio, quæ cum universali Lusitanorum, & Hispanorum, immo omnium fere fidelium scandalo hoc juramentum illicitum, & superstitiosum esse asserere non veretur. | | 139 It is likewise certain that this doctrine of Muratori has brought the greatest scandal to the whole Kingdom, to all the Spains; which indeed Sixtus IV studied in every way to avoid, through these words: *We therefore, wishing, so far as is granted us from on high, to meet such temerarious daring and perverse assertions as may thence arise in the Church of God, by our own motion.... [declare that] those who should presume to affirm that those who believed,* (note that he also prohibits affirming that those believing the immaculate Conception incur sin) *or held that the same Mother of God was preserved from the stain of original sin in her Conception, would on that account be defiled with the stain of some heresy, or sin mortally.... as false, and erroneous, and utterly alien from the truth, and the books published thereupon containing this, we by Apostolic authority, by the tenor of these presents, as to this reprobate and condemn.* And afterwards he brands with a sentence of excommunication reserved to the Apostolic See all who shall have dared to assert that the *sententiæ* concerning the immaculate Conception are not true. What else does Muratori attempt? He expressly asserts that it *can be false.* But he who affirms that it can be false necessarily denies that it is true; for that which is true is not subject to falsity. Furthermore, the Pontiff would not prohibit denying it to be true, if he judged that it could be false. Ridiculous, therefore, are those who hold that the Holy Pontiff constituted nothing else than to permit the aforesaid *sententia* as pious and probable; whereas he so judges it true that he so luminously prohibits asserting that it is not true. If therefore the *sententia* which defends the immaculate Conception of the Mother of God is true, and to be held as true, the oath concerning the defending of this truth is licit also: accordingly that sinister opinion is illicit, reprobated, and liable to Pontifical condemnation, which, with the universal scandal of the Portuguese and of the Spaniards, nay of almost all the faithful, does not fear to assert this oath to be illicit and superstitious. |
| 140 Severior adhuc est Alexandri VII. Constitutio, & quod legatur dignissima, saltem quoad partem nostræ opinioni expressissime faventem. Et notandum hanc constitutionem editam fuisse quando jam ab omnibus fere Universitatibus de tuenda Conceptione publicum præstabatur juramentum; neque tamen Pontifex illud prohibuit, neque consideravit *causam Ecclesiæ male agi*, & ipsi Ecclesiæ multum interesse hanc *superstitionem populo universo innotescere*; non superstitionem timuit, quam *timuisse, timereque* dicit Muratorius *Apostolicam Sedem*; neque in illo voto religionis periculum, & peccati reatum cogitavit, alias illud prohiberet, nullatenusque sineret eam superstitionem exerceri, & fidelium mentibus infixam radicari. Quod autem S. Pontifex nullatenus improbavit, explodit Muratorius. Videamus nunc Alexandri verba. Sic ait: *Solicitudo omnium Ecclesiarum, quam, licet meritis, & viribus longe impares, Dei optimi Maximi voluntate, & providentia gerimus, in id nos anxie tenet intentos, & vigilantes, ut scandala, quæ inter fideles pro humanæ naturæ corruptione, & fragilitate necesse est ut veniant, quantum fieri potest, paucissima exoriantur, atque exorta quam celerrime, & quam diligentissime amoveantur: nam iis, per quos veniunt, certam peccati perniciem, quibus verò præbentur, præsens afferunt labendi periculum,* (hanc sibi, & omnibus fere fidelibus perniciem adducere, sub imprudentissimi zeli specie, & superstitionis tollendæ larva, Muratorius non exhorruit) *quorum nos, pro nostro Pastoralis officii debito, & damnum summopere dolemus, & discrimine assiduo urimur. Sane vetus est Christi fidelium erga ejus Beatissimam Matrem Virginem Mariam pietas sentientium ejus animam in primo instanti creationis, atque infusionis in corpus fuisse speciali Dei gratia, & privilegio, intuitu meritorum JESU Christi ejus filii humani generis Redemptoris, à macula peccati originalis præservatam immunem, atque in hoc sensu ejus Conceptionis * festivitatem solemni ritu colentium, & celebrantium:* {marg. Nota} *crevitque horum numerus, atque hujusmodi cultus post editas à fel. rec. Xisto PP. IV. prædecessore nostro in ejus commendationem Apostolicas constitutiones, quas sacrum Concilium Tridentinum innovavit, atque observari mandavit. Aucta rursus, & propagata fuit pietas hæc, & cultus erga Deiparam post erecta hoc nomine, approbantibus Romanis Pontificibus, religiosum Ordinem, & confraternitates, ac concessas ab iisdem indulgentias,* (hæc est illa pia sententia, quam Muratorius in verbis, & *pro mendacio interdum*, temerarie, ne dicam blaspheme, mendacem esse posse proferre non addubitat) *ita ut accedentibus quoque plerisque celebrioribus Academiis* (plus sapere, meliusque his omnibus Academiis celebratissimis præsumit Muratorius, & definit *cum eo posteros nostros, si quidquam sapiant probaturos*) *jam omnes fere Catholici eam complectantur* (ab hoc Catholicorum numero excluditur Muratorius, dum asserit hanc opinionem meræ probabilitatis errori obnoxiæ terminos non transcendere, nec tanta contentione tenendum, *quod aliter Deus voluisse potest, incertumque adhuc est, an aliter esse voluerit.* Rara hominis devotio, rarissima pietatis indoles!) *Et quia occasione contrariæ assertionis in concionibus, lectionibus, conclusionibus, & actibus publicis, quod nempe Beatissima Virgo MARIA fuerit concepta cum peccato originali, oriebantur in populo Christiano cum magna Dei offensa scandala, jurgia, dissensiones* (nonne eadem scandala oriri possunt ex libro, in quo asseritur, non esse certum, Virginem Deiparam ab originali fuisse præservatam, neque id definiri posse?) *Rec. mem. Paulus PP. V. etiam prædecessor noster vetuit horum opinionem præfatæ sententiæ contrariam publice doceri, aut prædicari. Quam prohibitionem piæ mem. Gregorius PP. XV. similiter prædecessor noster ad privata etiam colloquia extendit, mandans insuper in favorem ejusdem sententiæ, ut in sacrosancto Missæ sacrificio, ac divino Officio celebrandis, tam publice, quam privatim, non alio, quam Conceptionis nomine * uti quicumque debeant.* {marg. Nota} *Nihilominus prout venerabiles Fratres Episcopi fere omnes Hispaniarum cum Ecclesiarum suarum Capitulis, datis ad nos literis, exposuerunt, accedente etiam insinuatione charissimi in Christo filii nostri Philippi earumdem Hispaniarum Regis Catholici, qui specialem super hoc misit ad nos Oratorem venerabilem Fratrem Ludovicum Episcopum Placentinum, per quem etiam delatæ fuerunt ad nos supplicationes Regnorum earumdem Hispaniarum, pergunt aliqui contrarii illius opinionis assertores contra præfatas prohibitiones tum privatim, tum publice præfatam sententiam aut impugnare, aut vellicare,* (quid aliud facit Muratorius cum incertam, & indefinibilem dicit?) *& favorem à Romanis Pontificibus cultui, & festo secundum illam præstito, ita interpretari, ut frustrentur; immo Ecclesiam Romanam huic sententiæ, & cultui juxta illam Beatæ Virgini exhibito favere negant,* (etiam hujus culpæ reus censendus est Muratorius. Et quomodo incerta censenda erit sententia, cui Ecclesia Romana favet?) *pios Christi fideles è sua quasi pacifica possessione deturbare conando,* (& quis dubitat Muratorium à nostra nos possessione pacifica, & immemoriali deturbare?) *unde offensiones, scandala, & jurgia, quibus obviare voluerunt Paulus V. & Gregorius XV. nostri prædecessores, perdurant adhuc,* (hæc omnia sedavit Alexander, sed Muratorius excitare cupit) *& ex occasione eorumdem adversantium majora his incommoda in posterum prudenter, & merito timentur. Quapropter super his tam præfati Episcopi cum Ecclesiarum suarum Capitulis, quam memoratus Philippus Rex, ejusque Regna nobis pro opportuno remedio instanter supplicari fecerunt. Nos considerantes, quod sancta Romana Ecclesia de intemeratæ, semperque Virginis MARIÆ Conceptione festum solemniter celebrat, & speciale, ac proprium super hoc Officium olim ordinavit juxta piam, devotam, & laudabilem institutionem, quæ à Xisto IV. prædecessore nostro tunc emanavit; volentesque laudabili huic pietati, & devotioni, & festo, ac cultui secundum illam exhibito in Ecclesia Romana post ipsius cultus institutionem nunquam immutato, Romanorum Pontificum prædecessorum nostrorum exemplo favere, necnon tueri pietatem, & devotionem hanc colendi, & celebrandi Beatissimam Virginem, præveniente scilicet Spiritus Sancti gratia à peccato originali præservatam,* (quod Ecclesia tam perspicuis constitutionibus tuetur, nos non poterimus juramento profiteri?) *cupientesque in Christi grege unitatem spiritus in vinculo pacis, sedatis offensionibus, & jurgiis, amotisque scandalis, conservare: ad præfatorum Episcoporum cum Ecclesiarum suarum Capitulis, ac Philippi Regis, ejusque Regnorum oblatam nobis instantiam, ac preces, constitutiones, & decreta à Romanis Pontificibus prædecessoribus nostris, & præcipue à Xisto IV. Paulo V. & Gregorio XV. edita in favorem sententiæ asserentis, animam B. Mariæ Virginis in sui creatione, & in corpus infusione Spiritus Sancti gratia donatam, & à peccato originali præservatam fuisse, necnon & in favorem festi, & cultus Conceptioni ejusdem Virginis Deiparæ, secundum piam istam sententiam, ut præfertur, exhibiti, innovamus, & sub censuris, & pœnis in iisdem constitutionibus contentis observari mandamus. Et insuper omnes, & singulos, qui præfatas constitutiones, seu decreta ita pergent interpretari, ut favorem per illas dictæ sententiæ, & festo secundum illam exhibito frustrentur, vel qui hanc eamdem sententiam, festum, seu cultum in disputationem revocare, aut contra eam quoquo modo directe, vel indirecte, aut sub quovis prætextu, etiam definibilitatis ejus examinandæ,* (quid aliud attentat Muratorius?) *sive S. Scripturam, aut SS. Patres, sive Doctores glossandi, vel interpretandi, denique alio quovis prætextu, seu occasione, scripto, seu voce loqui, concionari, tractare, disputare, contra ea quicquam determinando, aut asserendo, vel argumenta contra ea afferendo, & insoluta relinquendo, aut alio quovis excogitabili modo* (hunc modum excogitavit Muratorius directe contra juramentum, tamquam illicitum, & superstitiosum, subdole, & indirecte contra opinionem, supra quam cadit tale juramentum) *differendo ausi fuerint præter pœnas, & censuras &c.* Ubi specialiter notandum est, Tribunali Fidei subjicere, ut debitis pœnis puniantur, omnes, qui prædictam opinionem ausi fuerint quoquo modo improbare, aut ei aliquam notam inurere. Videat Muratorius, an aliud audeat, cum superstitiosum, & pervicax dicat juramentum hujus veritatis, & piissimæ sententiæ, hodieque ab omnibus fidelibus inconcusse retentæ, & sine aliqua formidine pro vera habitæ. His robur addit tum ipsius Alexandri VII. Decretum, quo jussit festum Conceptionis celebrari cum octava, tum Decretum Clementis XI. constituentis, quod idem festum sub præcepto celebretur. Quæ quidem ipsius purissimæ Conceptionis sanctitatem quam maxime suadent. Quis tam demens erit, qui huic sententiæ omnem moralem, & fidei proximam adimat certitudinem, hujusque veritatis tuendæ juramentum superstitiosum asserat, & illicitum? Solus Muratorius in celebriores Academias, christianissimaque Regna audens inconsulte nimis. | | 140 More severe still is the Constitution of Alexander VII, and one most worthy to be read, at least as regards the part most expressly favoring our opinion. And it is to be noted that this constitution was issued when already by almost all the Universities a public oath concerning the defending of the Conception was being taken; nor yet did the Pontiff prohibit it, nor did he consider that *the cause of the Church is ill served*, and that it greatly concerns the Church herself *that this superstition become known to the whole people*; he did not fear the superstition which Muratori says *the Apostolic See feared and fears*; nor did he think there was in that vow a danger to religion and the guilt of sin, otherwise he would prohibit it, and would in no wise suffer that superstition to be exercised, and to be rooted fixed in the minds of the faithful. But what the Holy Pontiff in no wise disapproved, Muratori explodes. Let us now see the words of Alexander. Thus he says: *The solicitude of all the Churches, which, though far unequal in merits and in strength, we bear by the will and providence of God the best and greatest, holds us anxiously intent and vigilant upon this: that the scandals which, by reason of the corruption and frailty of human nature, must needs come among the faithful, should arise as few as possible, and, having arisen, should be removed as swiftly and as diligently as possible: for to those through whom they come they bring a certain ruin of sin, but to those to whom they are offered a present danger of falling,* (that he was bringing this ruin upon himself and upon almost all the faithful, under the appearance of a most imprudent zeal and the mask of taking away superstition, Muratori did not shudder) *the loss whereof we, by the debt of our Pastoral office, both exceedingly lament, and are burned with assiduous peril. Ancient indeed is the piety of Christ's faithful toward his most Blessed Mother the Virgin Mary, holding that her soul, in the first instant of its creation and infusion into the body, was by a special grace and privilege of God, in view of the merits of JESUS Christ her Son the Redeemer of the human race, preserved immune from the stain of original sin, and in this sense honoring and celebrating with solemn rite the festivity of her Conception:* {marg. Nota} *and the number of these grew, and this cult likewise, after the Apostolic constitutions issued in its commendation by our predecessor Sixtus IV of happy memory, which the sacred Council of Trent renewed and commanded to be observed. Again was this piety and cult toward the Mother of God increased and propagated after the erection under this name, the Roman Pontiffs approving, of a religious Order and of confraternities, and the indulgences granted by the same,* (this is that pious *sententia* which Muratori, in words, and *at times for a lie*, temerariously — not to say blasphemously — does not hesitate to declare can be mendacious) *so that, very many of the more celebrated Academies also acceding,* (Muratori presumes to know more, and better, than all these most celebrated Academies, and defines *that our posterity, along with him, if they have any wisdom, will approve it*) *now almost all Catholics embrace it* (from this number of Catholics Muratori is excluded, while he asserts that this opinion does not transcend the terms of a mere probability liable to error, nor is to be held with so great contention, *since God could have willed otherwise, and it is still uncertain whether he willed it to be otherwise.* Rare devotion of the man, most rare disposition of piety!) *And because, on occasion of the contrary assertion in sermons, lectures, conclusions, and public acts — namely, that the Most Blessed Virgin MARY was conceived with original sin — there arose among the Christian people, with great offense to God, scandals, quarrels, dissensions* (can not the same scandals arise from a book in which it is asserted that it is not certain that the Virgin Mother of God was preserved from original sin, nor that this can be defined?) *Pope Paul V of happy memory, likewise our predecessor, forbade the opinion of these men, contrary to the aforesaid sententia, to be publicly taught or preached. Which prohibition Pope Gregory XV of pious memory, likewise our predecessor, extended also to private colloquies, commanding moreover, in favor of the same sententia, that in celebrating the most holy sacrifice of the Mass and the divine Office, both publicly and privately, all whosoever should use no other name than that of the Conception.* {marg. Nota} *Nevertheless, as the venerable Brethren the Bishops of almost all the Spains, with the Chapters of their Churches, have set forth in letters sent to us — with the added insinuation also of our most beloved son in Christ Philip, Catholic King of the same Spains, who upon this sent to us a special Orator, the venerable Brother Ludovicus, Bishop of Plasencia, through whom also were conveyed to us the supplications of the same Kingdoms of the Spains — certain assertors of that contrary opinion continue, against the aforesaid prohibitions, both privately and publicly, either to impugn or to pluck at the aforesaid sententia,* (what else does Muratori do, when he calls it uncertain and indefinable?) *and so to interpret the favor shown by the Roman Pontiffs to the cult and to the feast according to it, as to frustrate them; nay, they deny that the Roman Church favors this sententia, and the cult exhibited to the Blessed Virgin according to it,* (of this fault too Muratori must be reckoned guilty. And how shall a *sententia* be deemed uncertain, which the Roman Church favors?) *striving to dislodge the pious faithful of Christ from their, as it were, peaceful possession,* (and who doubts that Muratori dislodges us from our peaceful and immemorial possession?) *whence the offenses, scandals, and quarrels which Paul V and Gregory XV our predecessors wished to meet, endure still,* (all these Alexander settled, but Muratori desires to stir them up) *and on occasion of the same adversaries greater inconveniences than these are prudently and deservedly feared for the future. Wherefore upon these matters both the aforesaid Bishops with the Chapters of their Churches, and the aforementioned King Philip and his Kingdoms, have caused instant supplication to be made to us for an opportune remedy. We, considering that the holy Roman Church solemnly celebrates a feast of the Conception of the undefiled and ever Virgin MARY, and once ordained a special and proper Office upon this, according to the pious, devout, and laudable institution which then issued from our predecessor Sixtus IV; and wishing, after the example of the Roman Pontiffs our predecessors, to favor this laudable piety and devotion, and the feast and cult exhibited according to it — never changed in the Roman Church since the institution of that cult — and also to protect this piety and devotion of honoring and celebrating the Most Blessed Virgin as preserved from original sin by the prevenient grace of the Holy Spirit,* (which the Church protects by constitutions so perspicuous: shall we not be able to profess it by oath?) *and desiring to preserve in the flock of Christ the unity of spirit in the bond of peace, offenses and quarrels being settled and scandals removed: at the instance and prayers offered to us by the aforesaid Bishops with the Chapters of their Churches, and by King Philip and his Kingdoms, we renew the constitutions and decrees issued by the Roman Pontiffs our predecessors, and especially by Sixtus IV, Paul V, and Gregory XV, in favor of the sententia asserting that the soul of the Blessed Virgin Mary, in her creation and in her infusion into the body, was endowed with the grace of the Holy Spirit and preserved from original sin, and also in favor of the feast and cult exhibited to the Conception of the same Virgin Mother of God according to that pious sententia, as aforesaid; and we command them to be observed under the censures and penalties contained in the same constitutions. And moreover, all and singular who shall continue so to interpret the aforesaid constitutions or decrees as to frustrate the favor shown by them to the said sententia and to the feast exhibited according to it; or who shall have dared to call this same sententia, feast, or cult into disputation, or in any way whatsoever, directly or indirectly, or under any pretext whatsoever — even that of examining its definability,* (what else does Muratori attempt?) *or of glossing or interpreting Holy Scripture, or the Holy Fathers, or the Doctors; or finally under any other pretext or occasion whatsoever, in writing or by word, to speak, preach, treat, dispute, by determining or asserting anything against them, or by bringing forward arguments against them and leaving them unresolved, or in any other conceivable manner* (this manner Muratori has excogitated: directly against the oath, as illicit and superstitious; craftily and indirectly against the opinion upon which such an oath falls) *to defer them — besides the penalties and censures, etc.* Where it is specially to be noted that he subjects to the Tribunal of the Faith, that they may be punished with due penalties, all who shall have dared in any way to disapprove the aforesaid opinion, or to brand it with any mark. Let Muratori see whether he dares anything else, when he calls superstitious and obstinate the oath of this truth and most pious *sententia*, held today unshakenly by all the faithful, and held for true without any fear. To these things strength is added both by the Decree of Alexander VII himself, by which he ordered the feast of the Conception to be celebrated with an octave, and by the Decree of Clement XI establishing that the same feast be celebrated under precept. Which indeed most greatly persuade the sanctity of that most pure Conception. Who will be so demented as to take away from this *sententia* all moral certitude, and that proximate to faith, and to assert the oath for the defending of this truth to be superstitious and illicit? Muratori alone, daring far too inconsiderately against the more celebrated Academies and most Christian Kingdoms. |
| 141 Hæc sat essent. Sed ex superabundanti suadetur 2. Ex eo esset illicitum illud juramentum, quia prodige quis se devoveret pro tuenda veritate ab Ecclesia nondum definita. Hoc est fundamentum, cui Muratorius plurimum confidit. Atqui non prodige: ergo neque illicite. Ex dictis constat quænam sit Ecclesiæ circa immaculatam Conceptionem definitio, quæ sub juramento licite, & religiose cadat. Quod autem non prodige, ostenditur. Licitum est subdito pro tuendo Regis honore, & pro defendenda Regni possessione, quæ non modo probabiliori, sed jure moraliter certo, & cui falsum subesse nequit, fundatur, sanguinem fundere, vitamque certæ morti objicere, ut constat ex communi DD. asserentium posse, immo teneri milites in bello justo pugnare cum certo mortis periculo, stationem sibi à Duce assignatam non deserere; immo navim, & se ipsos incendere, ne victoria audaciores, & ditiores reddantur infideles, ut tenent Lessius *lib. 2. cap. 9. dub. 6. n. 34.* Bonacinas *disp. 2. q. ultim. punct. 5. num. 5.* Trullench *lib. 5. cap. 3. dub. 3. n. 11.* Malderus 2. 2. *tract. 3. cap. 1. dub. 19.* Fagundes *in Decalog. tom. 1. lib. 5. cap. 11. n. 14.* apud Salmanticenses *tract. 13. de Restit. cap. 2. punct. 2. §. 3. n. 37.* Ergo etiam non prodige, sed licite potest quis cum certo vitæ dispendio tueri honorem Beatissimæ Virginis asserendo immaculatam illius Conceptionem: qui quidem honor, utpote longe superioris ordinis, præstabilior est cunctis Regnis, ac ditionibus; maxime, quod in hoc solum detur remotum longe periculum, & indirecta sui occisio tantummodo reperiatur, quæ ex causa licet, & probant adducta, aliaque similia exempla. | | 141 These things would be enough. But from superabundance it is urged, secondly. On this ground would that oath be illicit: because one would prodigally devote himself for the defending of a truth not yet defined by the Church. This is the foundation in which Muratori most greatly trusts. But not prodigally: therefore neither illicitly. From what has been said it is evident what is the Church's definition concerning the immaculate Conception, which may licitly and religiously fall under an oath. But that it is not prodigal, is shown. It is licit for a subject, for the defending of the King's honor, and for the defending of the possession of the Kingdom — which is founded not merely upon the more probable, but upon a right morally certain, and to which falsity cannot underlie — to shed his blood, and to expose his life to certain death, as is evident from the common opinion of the Doctors asserting that soldiers can, nay are bound, to fight in a just war with certain danger of death, and not to desert the station assigned them by their Commander; nay, to set fire to their ship and to themselves, lest by victory the infidels be rendered more audacious and more wealthy, as Lessius holds *lib. 2. cap. 9. dub. 6. n. 34.*, Bonacina *disp. 2. q. ultim. punct. 5. num. 5.*, Trullench *lib. 5. cap. 3. dub. 3. n. 11.*, Malderus 2. 2. *tract. 3. cap. 1. dub. 19.*, Fagundes *in Decalog. tom. 1. lib. 5. cap. 11. n. 14.*, in the Salmanticenses *tract. 13. de Restit. cap. 2. punct. 2. §. 3. n. 37.* Therefore also not prodigally, but licitly, can one, with certain expenditure of life, defend the honor of the Most Blessed Virgin by asserting her immaculate Conception: which honor indeed, as being of a far superior order, is more excellent than all Kingdoms and dominions; especially since in this there is given only a remote and far-off danger, and only an indirect slaying of oneself is found, which for a cause is licit, and which the examples adduced, and others like them, prove. |
| 142 Neque hoc argumentum effugere poterit Muratorius asserendo, honorem B. Virginis non consistere *in dubiis laudibus, neque imprudenti sacrificio. Illi honorem, sed prudenter habendum,* atque cavendum, *ne amorem, ac reverentiam Virgini debitam superstitiosam efficiamus.* Dubiæ erunt Muratorio hæ laudes, nobis non sunt. Ipsi incerta est nostrarum laudationum causa, ut pro modulo suo, quanta dexteritate possit, Xisto, & Alexandro contradicat: nobis non est, ne Pontificibus ipsis contraire videamur, qui omnem nostræ piæ sententiæ contradictionem omnino prohibuerunt. Non imprudens dici potest nostrum sacrificium: quid enim imprudentiæ habere potest, quod votis nostris colamus illud ipsum, quod Ecclesia colit, eo modo, & eo sensu, quo illa colendum instituit. Non nos devovemus pro tuenda opinione mere humana, seu Scotistica, ut alii blaterant, & fingunt, sed pro tuenda sententia, licet nondum de fide definita, quam Ecclesia ut piam, & veriorem amplectitur, & amplectendam decernit. Non tendunt vota nostra ad opinionem illam, ut puram opinionem; sed ad colendam Matrem tam insigni honore, & privilegio decoratam, & ad colendum in Matre Filium, qui eam à communi omnium mortalium contagio immunem reddidit. Diriguntur ad obsequendum Ecclesiæ, ejusque supremo capiti jubenti nihil asserere, dubitare, determinareve contra sententiam Marianæ immunitati faventem. Huic expressissimo præcepto contradiceremus, si quandoque ab ea sententia discedere placuisset. Juramus ergo, & licite, prudenterque juramus firmiter stare pro sententia, quam Ecclesia evidenter recipit, eique nullatenus contraire, aut pro contraria quidquam determinare. Tandem id contendimus, ut sanctissimam Dei parentem in sua immemoriali possessione conservemus, neque nobis unquam liceat eam spoliare honore maximo, qualis proculdubio censendus est, quod ea inter omnes filias Adæ, sicut rosa inter spinas, illibata conciperetur. | | 142 Nor will Muratori be able to escape this argument by asserting that the honor of the Blessed Virgin does not consist *in doubtful praises, nor in an imprudent sacrifice. Honor is to be paid to her, but prudently,* and care must be taken *lest we render the love and reverence due to the Virgin superstitious.* These praises will be doubtful to Muratori; to us they are not. To him the cause of our praises is uncertain, that after his own measure, with what dexterity he can, he may contradict Sixtus and Alexander: to us it is not, lest we seem to go against the Pontiffs themselves, who altogether prohibited every contradiction of our pious *sententia*. Our sacrifice cannot be called imprudent: for what imprudence can there be in this, that by our vows we honor that very thing which the Church honors, in that manner, and in that sense, in which she instituted it to be honored. We do not devote ourselves for the defending of a merely human, or Scotistic, opinion, as others prate and feign, but for the defending of a *sententia* which, although not yet defined of faith, the Church embraces as pious and more true, and decrees to be embraced. Our vows tend not to that opinion, as a pure opinion; but to venerating the Mother adorned with so signal an honor and privilege, and to venerating in the Mother the Son, who rendered her immune from the common contagion of all mortals. They are directed to the obeying of the Church, and of her supreme head, who commands that nothing be asserted, doubted, or determined against the *sententia* favoring the Marian immunity. We should contradict this most express precept, if at any time it should have pleased us to depart from that *sententia*. We swear, therefore — and licitly and prudently do we swear — to stand firmly for the *sententia* which the Church evidently receives, and in no way to go against it, or to determine anything for the contrary. Finally, we strive for this, that we may preserve the most holy parent of God in her immemorial possession, and that it may never be lawful for us to despoil her of that greatest honor — and such without doubt must it be reckoned — that she, among all the daughters of Adam, like a rose among thorns, was conceived unsullied. |
| 143 Possidere autem B. Virginem præcellentissimum honorem immunitatis ab originali, evincitur ex traditione illius per singula Ecclesiæ sæcula, quam eruditissime probat singula recensendo P. Ferdinandus de Salazar *de Concept. supra citat. cap. 42. per totum.* Et sane ea est totius Hispaniæ traditio, quidquid critici aut ex contradictionis spiritu, aut ex invidia detrectent. Patet 2. ex fere communi Patrum sensu, de quo citatus Salazar, P. Velasques de Conceptione, Fr. Ægidius à Præsentatione, P. Vega in sua Theologia Mariana latissime id ostendentes. Quamvis autem æmuli isti Patrum loca pro libito interpretentur, in hoc ipso Alexandrinæ Constitutioni contraveniunt, ubi id prohibetur in verbis: *Sive S. Scripturam, aut SS. Patres, sive DD. glossandi vel interpretandi.* Patet 3. ex Tridentino *sess. 5. de Peccato Originali*, ubi licet non definiatur immunitas B. Virginis ab originali, tamen asseritur Virgo immaculata, & declarat Concilium suæ intentionis non esse eam comprehendere in Decreto, ubi de peccato originali agitur, confirmatque, & præcipit observari Constitutiones Xisti IV. Verba Concilii sic se habent: *Declarat tamen hæc sancta Synodus, non esse suæ intentionis, comprehendere in hoc Decreto, ubi de peccato originali agitur, Beatam, & immaculatam Virginem Mariam Dei Genitricem; sed observandas esse Constitutiones Sixti Papæ IV. sub pœnis in eis Constitutionibus contentis, quas innovat.* | | 143 But that the Blessed Virgin possesses the most excellent honor of immunity from original sin is evinced from the tradition thereof through each of the Church's centuries, which Fr. Ferdinandus de Salazar most eruditely proves, reviewing them one by one, *de Concept. supra citat. cap. 42. per totum.* And truly such is the tradition of all Spain, whatever the critics, whether out of a spirit of contradiction or out of envy, may detract. It is evident, secondly, from the almost common mind of the Fathers, concerning which the cited Salazar, Fr. Velasques de Conceptione, Fr. Ægidius a Præsentatione, and Fr. Vega in his Theologia Mariana show this most amply. But although those rivals interpret the passages of the Fathers at their pleasure, in this very thing they contravene the Alexandrine Constitution, where this is prohibited in the words: *Whether of glossing or interpreting Holy Scripture, or the Holy Fathers, or the Doctors.* It is evident, thirdly, from the Council of Trent, *sess. 5. de Peccato Originali*, where, although the immunity of the Blessed Virgin from original sin is not defined, nevertheless the Virgin is asserted to be immaculate, and the Council declares that it is not of its intention to comprehend her in the Decree where original sin is treated, and it confirms, and commands to be observed, the Constitutions of Sixtus IV. The words of the Council run thus: *This holy Synod declares, nevertheless, that it is not of its intention to comprehend in this Decree, where original sin is treated, the Blessed and Immaculate Virgin Mary, Mother of God; but that the Constitutions of Pope Sixtus IV are to be observed, under the penalties contained in those Constitutions, which it renews.* |
| 144 Nescio profecto quid expressius, aut quid definitioni proximius, quam definire Concilium Tridentinum omnes Adæ filios peccatum originale incurrisse, non tamen comprehendere in hoc Decreto Beatissimam Virginem, eamque nominare immaculatam, quod nomen, præter Christum, & Mariam, nulli alii Sancto invenitur concessum; quod certe Concilium non faceret, si eam singulari privilegio munitam non arbitraretur, nec agnosceret eam legem non pro ea, sed pro cæteris omnibus latam fuisse. Notandum vero est, non solum loqui Tridentinum de Constitutione Xistina, qua officium Conceptionis approbavit, illud recitantibus, festumque celebrantibus indulgentias concessit, sed de secundò edita, qua anathemate ferit asserentes, Ecclesiam celebrare festum sanctificationis, non vero Conceptionis B. Virginis. Ut autem apertius constet qualisnam fuerit mens Patrum Concilii circa immunitatem B. Virginis, & quare demum non prodierit illius definitio, audiatur P. Nicolaus Lancicius inclytæ Societatis JESU *tom. 2. opusc. 11. de Praxi divin. præsent. cap. 13. pag. 49.* qui tamquam testis oculatus ait: *Legi, & descripsi anno 1549. ex Actis originalibus Concilii Tridentini (quæ asservantur in Castro Sancti Angeli Romano, & cum expressa licentia Clementis VIII. Summi Pontificis R. P. Petro de Arrubal Professori Theologiæ in Collegio Romano, & mihi ejus socio ad aliquas horas exhibita à Cardinali Cæsio) ex aliquot Decadibus Episcoporum deputatorum ad tractandum de hoc puncto Conceptionis B. Virginis Mariæ, omnes asseruisse illam esse conceptam sine peccato originali; sed ob instantissimas preces aliquot Episcoporum, & Theologorum Sancti Dominici, qui illi Concilio aderant, rogantium, ne hac vice propter notam, quæ inureretur Ordini Sancti Dominici hoc Decreto, illud ederent, sed supersederent, & ad aliud tempus rejicerent. Concilium supersedit, & Decretum illud non vulgavit hanc solam ob causam. Ita habetur in illis Actis Concilii, quæ diligentissime legi, & hæc tunc excepi.* Hoc ipsum refert celebris Dominicanus, & Episcopus Minorensis, qui Concilio interfuit, in opusculo, quod scripsit de immaculata Conceptione, ubi refert etiam miraculum in se ipso expertum, ex quo motus fuit, ut Conceptionem defenderet, qui antea pro ea opinione non steterat, Ambrosius scilicet Catherinus. Idem ex iisdem actis testatur Doctor Hieronymus Ormachea *in Cant. Prolegom. 3.* verbo *Concilium Tridentinum.* | | 144 Truly I know not what could be more express, or what nearer to a definition, than that the Council of Trent should define that all the children of Adam have incurred original sin, yet should not comprehend in this Decree the Most Blessed Virgin, and should name her immaculate — a name which, besides Christ and Mary, is found granted to no other Saint; which surely the Council would not do, if it did not judge her fortified by a singular privilege, nor acknowledge that that law had been laid down not for her, but for all the rest. But it is to be noted that Trent speaks not only of the Sixtine Constitution by which he approved the office of the Conception, and granted indulgences to those reciting it and celebrating the feast, but of the second one issued, by which he strikes with anathema those asserting that the Church celebrates the feast of the sanctification, and not of the Conception, of the Blessed Virgin. But that it may more openly appear what was the mind of the Fathers of the Council concerning the immunity of the Blessed Virgin, and why in the end its definition did not come forth, let Fr. Nicolaus Lancicius of the illustrious Society of JESUS be heard, *tom. 2. opusc. 11. de Praxi divin. præsent. cap. 13. pag. 49.*, who as an eyewitness says: *I read, and copied out, in the year 1549, from the original Acts of the Council of Trent (which are preserved in the Roman Castle of Sant'Angelo, and which, with the express license of Clement VIII, Supreme Pontiff, were shown for some hours by Cardinal Cæsius to the Rev. Fr. Petrus de Arrubal, Professor of Theology in the Roman College, and to me, his companion), from several Decades of the Bishops deputed to treat of this point of the Conception of the Blessed Virgin Mary, that all asserted that she was conceived without original sin; but on account of the most insistent entreaties of some Bishops and Theologians of Saint Dominic, who were present at that Council, begging that this time, on account of the mark which would be branded upon the Order of Saint Dominic by this Decree, they should not issue it, but should hold off, and defer it to another time. The Council held off, and did not publish that Decree, for this sole cause. So it stands in those Acts of the Council, which I read most diligently, and these things I then extracted.* This same thing is related by the celebrated Dominican and Bishop of Minori, who took part in the Council, in the opuscule which he wrote on the Immaculate Conception, where he relates also a miracle experienced in his own person, by which he was moved to defend the Conception, though before he had not stood for that opinion — namely, Ambrosius Catharinus. The same is testified from the same Acts by Doctor Hieronymus Ormachea, *in Cant. Prolegom. 3.*, under the word *Concilium Tridentinum.* |
| 145 Scio Cardinalem Pallavicinum, apud aliquos magni historici nomine laudatus, aliter rem istam scripsisse, & in historia Concilii Tridentini *lib. 7. cap. 7.* asseruisse minime disputatum fuisse de immunitate B. Virginis, sed tantum de formula, qua à Decreto de peccato originali exciperetur eadem B. Virgo. Pallavicinus ille est, quem magni faciunt Franciscus Farvacques, Castoriensis, Bonus Merbesius, Gaspar Jueninus, Avermans, & alii ejusdem farinæ scriptores, quibus fortasse Muratorius accensendus sit: ille est, de quo magni nominis Theologi, inter quos Joannes de Launoy, & Maximilianus Le-Den, conqueruntur, ita suam historiam composuisse, ut ex Concilio excerperet quidquid suas sententias confirmare posset, aliqua vero reticuisset. Fides ea sit iis Doctoribus. Ego non capio cur potius Pallavicino standum sit, quam Lancicio, & Arrubali, & Catherino, & Ormacheæ rem sedulo examinantibus, & de visu deponentibus. Mirum tamen quod ipse ex Actis videret, quid Cardinalis *de Monte*, quid verò Cardinalis *Cervinus* de præservatione B. Virginis opinarentur, minime verum compertum haberet, quod alius Pauli III. Legatus Cardinalis Reginaldus *Sanctæ Mariæ in Cosmedin* sentiret, quæ quidem reticentia jam eum in mala fide constituit. Sed neque, si verum amamus, Pallavicinus, & Lancicius ex diametro opponuntur; nam ille solum refert contentionem inter duos Cardinales Legatos circa formam edendi Decreti de originali peccato; alter vero de Decadibus inter Episcopos habitis, & de unanimi omnium consensu circa immaculatam B. Virginis Conceptionem definiendam. Immo quod ipse Pallavicinus refert, plane ostendit Concilii Patres in ea esse sententia, Beatam scilicet Virginem in Decreto de peccato originali non esse comprehendendam, & solum dubitasse de formula, qua ea ab eo Decreto excipienda esset; quod quidem lippis tantumodo oculis non videbitur sat esse, ut Ecclesiæ circa immaculatam Conceptionem verus sensus evidenter appareat. Nescio quo fundamento tunc aliquot Episcopi restitissent formulæ, quam Pallavicinus refert, nempe: *Pie credi Virginem sine peccato originali conceptam.* Hæc enim formula, si rite perpendatur, non discrepat à Xistina Constitutione in eodem decreto confirmata, in qua proteguntur *qui crederent, aut tenerent eamdem Dei Genitricem ab originalis peccati macula in sua Conceptione præservatam fuisse.* Cum autem Xistus loquatur de hac pia credibilitate, non erat cur ab illis contradictoribus respueretur. Formula autem Concilii supra relata aliis concepta verbis eamdem continet, quatenus observandas Constitutiones Xistinas expresse decrevit. | | 145 I know that Cardinal Pallavicino, praised among some with the name of a great historian, wrote this matter otherwise, and in his history of the Council of Trent, *lib. 7. cap. 7.*, asserted that there was no dispute at all about the immunity of the Blessed Virgin, but only about the formula by which the same Blessed Virgin should be excepted from the Decree on original sin. That Pallavicino is he whom Franciscus Farvacques, Castoriensis, Bonus Merbesius, Gaspar Jueninus, Avermans, and other writers of the same leaven hold in high esteem, to whom perhaps Muratori is to be reckoned in addition: he it is of whom Theologians of great name, among whom Joannes de Launoy and Maximilianus Le-Den, complain that he so composed his history as to excerpt from the Council whatever could confirm his own opinions, while keeping silence on certain things. Let the credit of that rest with those Doctors. I do not grasp why we should stand by Pallavicino rather than by Lancicius, and Arrubal, and Catharinus, and Ormachea, who examined the matter with diligence and depose as eyewitnesses. Yet it is strange that he himself should see from the Acts what Cardinal *de Monte*, and what Cardinal *Cervinus*, opined concerning the preservation of the Blessed Virgin, yet should have found nothing at all ascertained as to what the other Legate of Paul III, Cardinal Reginaldus of *Sancta Maria in Cosmedin*, thought — which reticence indeed already sets him in bad faith. But neither, if we love the truth, are Pallavicino and Lancicius diametrically opposed; for the former relates only the contention between the two Cardinal Legates about the form of issuing the Decree on original sin; the latter, the Decades held among the Bishops, and the unanimous consent of all concerning the defining of the Immaculate Conception of the Blessed Virgin. Nay, what Pallavicino himself relates plainly shows that the Fathers of the Council were of this *sententia*, namely that the Blessed Virgin was not to be comprehended in the Decree on original sin, and that they doubted only about the formula by which she was to be excepted from that Decree; which indeed only to bleary eyes will fail to seem enough for the true mind of the Church concerning the Immaculate Conception to appear evidently. I know not on what foundation some Bishops would then have resisted the formula which Pallavicino relates, namely: *That the Virgin is piously believed to have been conceived without original sin.* For this formula, if it be rightly weighed, does not differ from the Sixtine Constitution confirmed in the same decree, in which protection is given to *those who should believe or hold that the same Mother of God was preserved from the stain of original sin in her Conception.* But since Sixtus speaks of this pious credibility, there was no reason why it should be spurned by those contradictors. And the formula of the Council related above, conceived in other words, contains the same, inasmuch as it expressly decreed that the Sixtine Constitutions were to be observed. |
| 146 Utut sit: Pallavicinus ipse fatetur, quod eamdem formulam non tulerunt *i Vescovi, e gli altri, che intervivano dell' Ordine Dominicano, ed ebbero dé seguaci*; in quo Lancicio cohæret. Obstiterunt ii Episcopi, & Theologi propter notam, quæ Ordini suo inureretur, & propter D. Thomæ opinionem, quam accerrime tuebantur, & quam ipse sequutus fuerat, ut se conformaret sententiæ, quæ tunc in Parisiensi Universitate versabatur; ut ipse S.D. cuidam devoto suo, Fratri nempe Dominico Rasola, Discalceatorum Carmelitarum Generali, viro virtutibus eximio, apparens, hanc suæ sententiæ causam aperuit, insimulque veritatem immaculatæ Conceptionis professus est, & venturum esse tempus, quo immaculata Conceptio definiretur. Certe nunc non obstitissent, præcipue Hispani, & Lusitani, qui idem juramentum in Universitatibus, ut gradum accipiant, libentissime emittunt. Et præcipue nullatenus obstabunt, si recordentur, eos fuisse ex primis cum suo sanctissimo Patriarcha Dominico, qui B. Virginem ab omni macula præservatam fuisse proclamarunt. Res scitu digna. Siquidem, cum idem sanctissimus Patriarcha, qui prius fuerat Ordinis S. Jacobi Eques, suum præclarissimum Ordinem instituisset, sicut multas Jacobei Ordinis cæremonias ad suum novum Ordinem invexerat, (ut sunt Officium parvum B. Virginis astantes recitare; à salutatione Angelica Missam incipere; non dextera, sed læva manu communicare, & similes aliæ) ita etiam Breviarium, & Missale Jacobeum observandum assignavit, ut de facto per sæcula observasse fertur. In præfato igitur tum Jacobei primum, tum postea Dominicani Ordinis Missali, sicut hodie festa B. Virginis Præfationem habent propriam, sic tunc proprium habebant Hymnum *Gloria in excelsis*; in festis enim Deiparæ sanctissimæ, ubi Hymnus habet *Filius Patris*, addebatur *Primogenitus Mariæ Virginis Matris.* Ubi habet: *Suscipe deprecationem nostram*, adjiciebatur *ad Mariæ gloriam.* Ubi habet: *Tu solus sanctus, tu solus Dominus, tu solus Altissimus*, sic legebatur: *Tu solus sanctus Mariam sanctificans, tu solus Dominus Mariam gubernans, tu solus Altissimus Mariam coronans.* In festo autem Conceptionis sic canebatur: *Tu solus sanctus Mariam præservasti: Tu solus Dominus Mariam fabricasti: Tu solus Altissimus Mariam sublimasti.* Adeo antiqua est hujus Ordinis Militaris erga Deiparæ purissimam Conceptionem mirifica devotio, fortasse à suo, & Hispaniarum Patrono per traditionem accepta, quidquid Hispanis traditionibus exteri Scriptores nimium detrahant. Adeo inclyti Dominicani Ordinis antiqua est tuendæ Conceptionis obligatio. Qui in hac materia plura scire cupiat, quibus meus Jacobeus, tum etiam meus charissimus Dominicanus Ordo nobilitantur, adeat Apologiam pro habitu Sancti Dominici in Ordine D. Jacobi typis datam Hispano sermone anno 1725. & scriptam per D. Josephum Lopes Arguleta, ubi late rem omnem discutit, comprobat, & ostendit Sanctum Patriarcham Dominicum Conceptionis mysterium prædicasse. Idemque tenet insignis Dogmatista Duvallius *in Apice certitudinis Theologicæ*, & doctissimus Nieremberg in libro *Exceptiones Tridentini pro omnimoda Deiparæ puritate cap. 22.* Nigarol. Bernardinus de Bustos, & alii, apud quos in ejus vitæ exemplaribus habentur ejusdem Sancti Patriarchæ hæc verba: *Sicut primus Adam fuit ex terra virgine, & nunquam maledicta formatus, ita decuit in secundo Adam fieri.* Hæc obiter attigisse libuit, ne hoc præclarissimi Ordinis illustre præconium silentio prætermitterem, suamque ab origine sententiam propalarem. His assensum non dabit Muratorius, omnem suæ sententiæ non faventibus fidem negare assuetus; sed ego non surdis auribus, sed piis lectoribus has notitias commendo. | | 146 However that may be: Pallavicino himself confesses that the same formula was not endured by *the Bishops, and the others of the Dominican Order who took part, and they had followers* [?]; in which he coheres with Lancicius. Those Bishops and Theologians stood in the way on account of the mark which would be branded upon their Order, and on account of the opinion of St. Thomas, which they defended most keenly, and which he himself had followed in order to conform himself to the *sententia* which was then current in the University of Paris; as the same holy Doctor, appearing to a certain devotee of his, namely Friar Dominicus Rasola, General of the Discalced Carmelites, a man of eximious virtues, disclosed this cause of his opinion, and at the same time professed the truth of the Immaculate Conception, and that a time would come in which the Immaculate Conception would be defined. Certainly now they would not have stood in the way, especially the Spaniards and the Portuguese, who most gladly take the same oath in the Universities, in order to receive a degree. And especially they will in no wise stand in the way, if they remember that they, with their most holy Patriarch Dominic, were among the first who proclaimed that the Blessed Virgin was preserved from every stain. A thing worth the knowing. For when the same most holy Patriarch, who had earlier been a Knight of the Order of St. James, had instituted his own most renowned Order, just as he had carried over many ceremonies of the Jacobean Order into his new Order (such as are: to recite the Little Office of the Blessed Virgin standing; to begin Mass from the Angelic Salutation; to communicate not with the right, but with the left hand; and other like customs), so also he assigned the Jacobean Breviary and Missal to be observed, as in fact it is reported to have observed them for centuries. In the aforesaid Missal, therefore — first of the Jacobean, and then afterwards of the Dominican Order — just as today the feasts of the Blessed Virgin have their proper Preface, so then they had a proper Hymn *Gloria in excelsis*; for on the feasts of the most holy Mother of God, where the Hymn has *Son of the Father*, there was added *the First-born of Mary the Virgin Mother.* Where it has: *Receive our prayer*, there was adjoined *unto the glory of Mary.* Where it has: *Thou alone art holy, Thou alone art Lord, Thou alone art Most High*, it was read thus: *Thou alone art holy, sanctifying Mary; Thou alone art Lord, governing Mary; Thou alone art Most High, crowning Mary.* But on the feast of the Conception it was sung thus: *Thou alone, the Holy One, hast preserved Mary: Thou alone, the Lord, hast fashioned Mary: Thou alone, the Most High, hast exalted Mary.* So ancient is the wondrous devotion of this Military Order toward the most pure Conception of the Mother of God, received perhaps by tradition from its own Patron, who is also the Patron of the Spains, whatever foreign Writers may too much detract from Spanish traditions. So ancient is the obligation of the illustrious Dominican Order to defend the Conception. Whoever desires to know more in this matter, whereby my Jacobean, and likewise my most dear Dominican Order, are ennobled, let him go to the Apology for the habit of Saint Dominic in the Order of St. James, given to the press in the Spanish tongue in the year 1725, and written by D. Josephus Lopes Arguleta, where he discusses the whole matter at length, confirms it, and shows that the holy Patriarch Dominic preached the mystery of the Conception. And the same is held by the distinguished Dogmatist Duvallius, *in Apice certitudinis Theologicæ*, and the most learned Nieremberg in the book *Exceptiones Tridentini pro omnimoda Deiparæ puritate cap. 22.*, Nigarol., Bernardinus de Bustos, and others, in whose copies of his life these words of the same holy Patriarch are found: *As the first Adam was formed from virgin earth, and never accursed, so it was fitting that it should be done in the second Adam.* These things it has pleased me to touch upon in passing, lest I pass over in silence this illustrious title of praise of that most renowned Order, and that I might publish abroad its *sententia* from its origin. To these things Muratori will not give his assent, accustomed as he is to deny all credit to those who do not favor his own opinion; but I commend these notices not to deaf ears, but to pious readers. |
| 147 Suadetur 3. ex Synodo Hierolomitana anno 1672. adversus Calvinistas, in quo interfuerunt 43. Patres, quæ sic habet: *Nullam è contagione peccati mortis suæ causam habuit Virgo sanctissima, sed ex ipsis dumtaxat naturæ principiis, quibus vel ante peccatum homo constabat; morti quippe suapte natura obnoxius erat ille, quamvis optimæ. Id vero beneficii Deus illi contulit, ut in eo nequaquam operante natura, sed gratia, immortalitatem sibi conciliaret. Hoc ipsum, & sanctissimæ Deiparæ contigit, quæ etsi bonis affluebat, ac peccatum nesciebat vel minimum, eadem illa, quæ morti addicunt hominem, naturæ elementa gestabat, ac pariter subinde mortalis erat. Verum eam à Deo gratiam obtinuit, ut mortem, si ita voluisset, nunquam obiret, sed cœlo etiamnum vitæ compos inferretur, hacce tamen prærogativa uti noluit.* Ex quibus verbis clare liquet, Patres illos sensisse B. Virginem ab omni originalis culpæ contagione fuisse liberam. Sicut enim Adam in statu justitiæ originalis ex solis naturæ principiis morti obnoxius esset; ita B. Maria in eodem statu ex solis ipsis principiis mortalis esset; & sicut Adam in eodem statu ex privilegio gratiæ immortalitatem assequeretur, ita B. Virgo eodem privilegio à morte immunis fieret, ni potius vellet morti subjici. | | 147 It is urged, thirdly, from the Synod of Jerusalem in the year 1672 against the Calvinists, in which 43 Fathers took part, which has as follows: *The most holy Virgin had no cause of her death from the contagion of sin, but only from those principles of nature of which man consisted even before sin; for he was by his very nature liable to death, although that nature was of the best. But God conferred upon him this benefit, that in this, nature in no wise working, but grace, he should win immortality for himself. This same thing befell also the most holy Mother of God, who, although she abounded in good things, and knew not sin even in the least, bore those same elements of nature which consign man to death, and likewise accordingly was mortal. But she obtained from God this grace, that she should never undergo death, if she had so willed, but should be borne into heaven while still possessed of life; yet she willed not to use this prerogative.* From which words it is clearly evident that those Fathers held that the Blessed Virgin was free from all contagion of original guilt. For as Adam in the state of original justice would be liable to death from the principles of nature alone, so Blessed Mary in the same state would be mortal from those same principles alone; and as Adam in the same state would attain immortality by a privilege of grace, so the Blessed Virgin by the same privilege would be made immune from death, had she not rather willed to be subjected to death. |
| 148 Suadetur 4. ex S. Pio V. & Gregorio XIII. damnantibus propositionem 69. Michaelis Baii sic dicentis: *Nemo præter Christum est absque peccato. Hinc B. Virgo mortua est propter peccatum ex Adam contractum, omnesque ejus afflictiones in hac vita, sicut & aliorum justorum, fuerunt ultiones vel peccati actualis, vel originalis.* Hæ, & aliæ propositiones his censuris confixæ sunt: *Quas quidem sententias hæreticas, erroneas, suspectas, temerarias, scandalosas, & impias, auribus offensionem immittentes respective auctoritate præsentium damnamus, & circumscribimus, & abolemus.* Neque dici potest, memoratam propositionem ideo damnatam, quia asserebat afflictiones hujus vitæ, quas B. Virgo passa est, *esse ultiones actualis, vel originalis peccati.* In quo videtur innui, B. Virginem non solum originali, sed actuali peccato fuisse obnoxiam. Nam contra est 1. quod damnatio est generalis, & absoluta totius propositionis, in qua principale assumptum erat, quod *B. Virgo mortua est propter peccatum ex Adam contractum.* Contra est 2. quia Baii propositio est disjunctiva, quæ ne falsa sit, sufficit quod altera ejus pars veritate nitatur. Ergo Pontifices damnando absolute talem propositionem, utramque illius partem damnare videntur. | | 148 It is urged, fourthly, from St. Pius V and Gregory XIII condemning proposition 69 of Michael Baius, which says thus: *No one besides Christ is without sin. Hence the Blessed Virgin died on account of the sin contracted from Adam, and all her afflictions in this life, as also those of the other just, were punishments of either actual or original sin.* These, and other propositions, were transfixed with these censures: *Which opinions, as respectively heretical, erroneous, suspect, rash, scandalous, and impious, bringing offense to ears, we by the authority of these presents condemn, and circumscribe, and abolish.* Nor can it be said that the aforesaid proposition was condemned for this reason, that it asserted that the afflictions of this life which the Blessed Virgin suffered *were punishments of actual, or of original sin.* In which it seems to be intimated that the Blessed Virgin was liable not only to original, but to actual sin. For against this stands, first, that the condemnation is general and absolute, of the whole proposition, in which the principal assertion was that *the Blessed Virgin died on account of the sin contracted from Adam.* Against it stands, secondly, that Baius's proposition is disjunctive; and, that it be not false, it suffices that one of its parts rest upon truth. Therefore the Pontiffs, by condemning such a proposition absolutely, are seen to condemn both of its parts. |
| 149 Suadetur 5. ex Decreto Gregorii XV. 24. Maii 1622. silentium indicenti tum in publicis, tum in privatis sermonibus, & scriptis *audentibus* asserere B. Virginem fuisse conceptam cum peccato originali: quod de publicis concionibus, lectionibus, ac disputationibus jam ante à Paulo V. cautum fuerat, & postea ab Alexandro VII. confirmatum. Ubi notandum, quod cum toties à Summis Pontificibus silentium imponatur asserentibus B. Virginem fuisse conceptam cum peccato originali, neutiquam hoc silentium indicatur asserentibus, defendentibus, aut prædicantibus eamdem B. Virginem ab originali culpa fuisse præservatam; immo ea sententia tot gratiis, indulgentiis, privilegiis, & constitutionibus foveatur. Hæc est sententia, quam Muratorius dicit terminos opinionis piæ non excedere, falsitati subesse posse, & indefinibilem prope esse. Mira profecto hominis istius in B. Virginem devotionis, & pietatis demonstratio! Quis prudens ejus larvas non ex primo prospectu intuens, sed medullitus introspiciens, ejus subdolum scribendi genus, ejusque animum minime pium non agnoscet? | | 149 It is urged, fifthly, from the Decree of Gregory XV of 24 May 1622, enjoining silence, both in public and in private discourses and writings, upon those *daring* to assert that the Blessed Virgin was conceived with original sin: which, as regards public sermons, lectures, and disputations, had already before been provided by Paul V, and was afterwards confirmed by Alexander VII. Where it is to be noted that, whereas silence is so often imposed by the Supreme Pontiffs upon those asserting that the Blessed Virgin was conceived with original sin, in no wise is this silence enjoined upon those asserting, defending, or preaching that the same Blessed Virgin was preserved from original guilt; nay rather, that *sententia* is fostered by so many graces, indulgences, privileges, and constitutions. This is the *sententia* which Muratori says does not exceed the bounds of a pious opinion, can be subject to falsity, and is well-nigh indefinable. A wondrous demonstration indeed of that man's devotion and piety toward the Blessed Virgin! What prudent man, gazing upon his masks not at first view, but inspecting them to the marrow, will not recognize his crafty manner of writing, and his mind by no means pious? |
| 150 Suadetur 6. ex Patribus Concilii Basiliensis *sess. 36.* qui communi suffragio B. Virginem à peccato originali immunem fuisse decreverunt, ejusque festum in tota Ecclesia celebrari constituerunt. Sic habet *sess. 36. Nos vero diligenter inspectis auctoritatibus, & rationibus, quæ jam à pluribus annis in publicis relationibus ex parte utriusque doctrinæ coram hac sacrosancta Synodo allegatæ sunt, aliisque etiam plurimis super hac re visis, & matura consideratione pensatis, doctrinam illam disserentem gloriosam Virginem Dei Genitricem MARIAM præveniente, & operante divini numinis gratia singulari nunquam actualiter subjacuisse originali peccato; sed immunem semper fuisse ab omni originali, & actuali culpa, sanctamque, & immaculatam; tamquam piam, & consonam cultui ecclesiastico, fidei Catholicæ, rectæ rationi, & sacræ Scripturæ, ab omnibus Catholicis approbandam fore, tenendam, & amplectendam definimus, & declaramus, nullique de cætero licitum esse in contrarium prædicare, seu docere &c.* Quæ quidem illorum Patrum sententiæ referuntur in historia Concilii Florentini *tom. 9.* Conciliorum per hæc verba: *Rebus igitur diligenter examinatis, publicam sessionem celebrant trigesimam sextam ad XV. Kalendas Octobris ejusdem anni, in qua solemni more declarant, gloriosam Virginem Mariam Dei Genitricem, præveniente, & operante divini numinis gratia singulari, nunquam actualiter subjacuisse originali peccato, sed immunem semper fuisse ab omni originali, & actuali culpa, sanctam, atque immaculatam: & hanc doctrinam ab omnibus approbandam, tenendamque judicant, statuentes nulli omnino deinceps licere in contrarium aliquid prædicare, seu asserere. Cujus festivitatem sexto Idus Decembris celebrandam more Romanæ Ecclesiæ comprobarunt.* Hinc miror doctissimum Natalem Alexandrum asseruisse Patres Concilii nihil aliud decernere, nisi eam sententiam piam esse; cum tamen ex utroque contextu liquido appareat eam approbasse ut consonam fidei Catholicæ, & sacræ Scripturæ, jussisseque ab omnibus teneri. Non intendimus per hoc aliquam veri Concilii authoritatem definitivam Basiliensi Synodo attribuere; quippe qui optime scimus, eam Synodum, licet à principio legitimam, tamquam à S.P. Martino V. indictam, postea illegitimam factam fuisse, atque, utpote tempore schismatis celebratam, definitionis pondere fuisse destitutam. Demonstramus tamen qualis omnium illorum Patrum mens fuerit de immunitate B. Virginis. Adnotatum tamen volumus, falso à quibusdam asseri decreta omnia Concilii Basiliensis fuisse reprobata, & omnes Theologos sanctæ Sedi addictos ab iis recessisse. Rejecit Eugenius IV.
